and `gguf_backend_description` can disagree**\n\n`has_gpu()` returns `true` for any non-CPU device in `gguf_devices`, including backends not explicitly recognized (e.g. `HIP`, `SYCL`, `OpenCL`). But `gguf_acceleration_labels` only checks `has_metal`, `has_cuda`, and `has_vulkan`, so for an unrecognised backend the labels list will be empty and `gguf_backend_description` will return `\"Cross-platform, CPU inference\"` — even though `sys.has_gpu()` is `true` and the device is listed in `ggufDevices` in the same JSON response.\n\nThe test `test_from_devices_handles_unknown_gpu_backends` already confirms the gap: a `ROCm` device yields `has_gpu()=true` but all three named flags are `false`. A frontend consuming the `system_info` response could therefore show `hasGpu: true` alongside `description: \"CPU inference\"`, which is contradictory.\n\nA simple guard in `gguf_backend_description` (and `gguf_backend_note`) to fall back to a generic \"GPU acceleration\" label when `has_gpu()` is true but no named flags are set would close this discrepancy:\n\n```rust\nfn gguf_backend_description(sys: &local_gguf::system_info::SystemInfo) -> String {\n    match gguf_acceleration_name(sys) {\n        Some(acceleration) => format!(\"Cross-platform, {acceleration} GPU acceleration\"),\n        None if sys.has_gpu() => \"Cross-platform, GPU acceleration\".to_string(),\n        None => \"Cross-platform, CPU inference\".to_string(),\n    }\n}\n```\n","created_at":"2026-03-11T21:11:27Z","updated_at":"2026-03-11T21:11:30Z","html_url":"https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/408#discussion_r2921023675","pull_request_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/pulls/408","_links":{"self":{"href":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/pulls/comments/2921023675"},"html":{"href":"https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/408#discussion_r2921023675"},"pull_request":{"href":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/pulls/408"}},"reactions":{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/pulls/comments/2921023675/reactions","total_count":0,"+1":0,"-1":0,"laugh":0,"hooray":0,"confused":0,"heart":0,"rocket":0,"eyes":0},"start_line":986,"original_start_line":727,"start_side":"RIGHT","line":990,"original_line":731,"side":"RIGHT","author_association":"CONTRIBUTOR","original_position":31,"position":31,"subject_type":"line"},{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/pulls/comments/2921023726","pull_request_review_id":3932563632,"id":2921023726,"node_id":"PRRC_kwDOREW6tc6uG0ju","diff_hunk":"@@ logging when `BackendAlreadyInitialized` triggers device listing without a guard**\n\nWhen `LlamaBackend::init()` returns `BackendAlreadyInitialized`, `backend_guard` is `None` and `list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()` is called without holding any guard. This is safe as long as the existing backend isn't freed concurrently, but it is a latent assumption about llama-cpp-2's initialization semantics. A `debug!` log at this branch (similar to the error branch) would make the code path observable and help diagnose unexpected results if the device list turns out empty:\n\n```rust\nErr(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n    debug!(\"llama backend already initialized; reusing for GGUF device probe\");\n    None\n},\n```\n\n\n<sub>Note: If this suggestion doesn't match your team's coding style, reply to this and let me know. Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Retain backend ownership when init reports already initialized**\n\nWhen two probes run concurrently, one call can get `Ok(backend)` while another hits `BackendAlreadyInitialized` and stores `None`; the second call still proceeds to `list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()` without any guard keeping the backend alive. Because the first call drops its guard at the end (which frees llama backend state), the second probe can race with teardown and return flaky device results or crash under concurrent `system_info` requests. Keep a shared lifetime/refcount for the backend (or avoid freeing it between probes) before listing devices.\n\nUseful? every system-info probe, and when it wins the global init race, concurrent GGUF model loads can fail with `BackendAlreadyInitialized` because both `local_llm::backend::GgufBackend::from_config` and `local_gguf::LocalGgufProvider::from_config` still treat that error as fatal. This makes local model startup flaky whenever a `system_info` request overlaps first-load initialization (for example, setup UI polling while a chat request triggers lazy load). Fresh evidence: this commit introduced the new init call here but did not add any shared backend guard or retry path in the existing loaders.\n\nUseful? Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Replace unavailable glslc package in Ubuntu 22.04 jobs**\n\nThe `rust-ci`/`rust-full-targets` workflow sections run inside `nvidia/cuda:12.4.1-devel-ubuntu22.04`, and this newly added `apt-get install ... glslc` step will fail on Jammy before clippy/tests execute because Jammy ships `glslang-tools` (with `glslangValidator`) but not a `glslc` package name. This regression blocks CI/release validation paths that previously passed; use a Jammy-available package (or a newer base image) for these jobs and mirror that fix in the matching `release.yml` install step.\n\nUseful? [{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/issues/comments/4042239290","html_url":"https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/408#issuecomment-4042239290","issue_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/moltis-org/moltis/issues/408","id":4042239290,"node_id":"IC_kwDOREW6tc7w7606","user":{"login":"greptile-apps[bot]","id":165735046,"node_id":"BOT_kgDOCeDqhg","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/in/867647?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D","html_url":"https://github.com/apps/greptile-apps","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/greptile-apps%5Bbot%5D/received_events","type":"Bot","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"created_at":"2026-03-11T21:11:24Z","updated_at":"2026-03-11T21:11:24Z","body":"<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>\n\nThis PR adds an opt-in `local-llm-vulkan` Cargo feature that threads the Vulkan GPU acceleration path through `moltis-providers`, `moltis-gateway`, and the CLI. The core detection mechanism is refactored from compile-time `cfg!` checks and a direct Metal FFI call to a unified llama.cpp runtime device probe (`list_llama_ggml_backend_devices`) encapsulated in the new `runtime_devices` module. The `system_info` RPC now returns richer device metadata (`ggufDevices`, `hasVulkan`) and the backend description/note strings are generalised to handle multiple concurrent acceleration labels.\n\n**Key changes:**\n- New `crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs` module encapsulates runtime GPU detection and provides `GgufRuntimeSupport` / `GgufRuntimeDevice` types.\n- Both `local_gguf/system_info.rs` and `local_llm/system_info.rs` are updated to derive GPU flags from runtime probing rather than compile-time feature flags, and both now carry a `gguf_devices` list and `has_vulkan` flag.\n- `local_llm_setup.rs` gains helper functions (`gguf_acceleration_labels`, `gguf_backend_description`, `gguf_backend_note`) and exposes `hasVulkan` + `ggufDevices` in the system-info RPC response.\n- `libvulkan-dev` and `glslc` are installed in all Linux `--all-features` CI/release Clippy jobs so the opt-in feature compiles in CI.\n\n**Issues found:**\n- `gguf_backend_description` (and `gguf_backend_note`) fall through to `\"CPU inference\"` whenever `has_metal`, `has_cuda`, and `has_vulkan` are all false — even when `has_gpu()` returns `true` for an unrecognised backend (e.g. HIP/SYCL). This produces a contradictory response where `hasGpu: true` and `description: \"CPU inference\"` appear together.\n- `read_proc_meminfo` / `parse_meminfo_kb` are now duplicated verbatim between `local_gguf/system_info.rs` and `local_llm/system_info.rs`; both `SystemInfo` structs are also structurally identical. Moving shared helpers into `runtime_devices.rs` would avoid drift.\n\n<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>\n\n- Mostly safe but has a logic inconsistency in user-facing descriptions that could confuse users with non-Metal/CUDA/Vulkan GPU backends.\n- The feature wiring, CI changes, and documentation are all correct. The main concern is the logic gap in gguf_backend_description: when a GPU device with an unrecognised backend is present, has_gpu() returns true while the description claims \"CPU inference\", which is misleading in the UI. The duplicated read_proc_meminfo helpers are a maintenance debt but not a correctness issue.\n- crates/gateway/src/local_llm_setup.rs — the gguf_backend_description / gguf_backend_note helper functions need a fallback for unrecognised GPU backends.\n\n<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>\n\n\n\n\n| Filename | Overview |\n|----------|----------|\n| crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs | New module that probes llama.cpp for runtime GPU devices. Logic is well-structured and tested. Minor concern: no diagnostic log in the BackendAlreadyInitialized branch, and has_gpu() includes unrecognised backends (e.g. ROCm) that the description helpers do not handle. |\n| crates/gateway/src/local_llm_setup.rs | New description helpers and system_info RPC updates look good overall, but gguf_backend_description/gguf_backend_note return \"CPU inference\" when an unrecognised GPU backend is present, contradicting has_gpu()=true in the same JSON response. |\n| crates/providers/src/local_gguf/system_info.rs | Metal FFI probe correctly replaced with runtime device detection. HQ","description":"## What is [CodSpeed](https://codspeed.io)?\r\nCodSpeed is a **continuous benchmarking** platform that allows you to **track** and **compare** the **performance** of your codebase during development.\r\n\r\nIt uses a smart **runtime engine** to measure the performance of your code in an **accurate** and **reproducible** way without creating a huge **runtime overhead**, unlike traditional benchmarks.\r\n\r\nCodSpeed produces detailed **performance reports** helping you **improve** your codebase **performance**, directly **within your repository provider** (Pull Requests comments, Merge checks, ...).\r\n\r\n## How it works\r\n\r\nThe core of the **performance measurement** is done directly within your **CI environment** through the **CodSpeed Action**. Allowing you to **stay the sole owner** of your data and to keep your **codebase private**.\r\n\r\nOnce the performance data is generated, **only the benchmark results** are sent to the CodSpeed servers to be **analyzed**.\r\n\r\nThen, performance reports are **generated** and **published** directly **within your repository provider**.\r\n\r\n## How long does it take to install?\r\n\r\nIf you're **already benchmarking** your codebase, you can plug your **existing benchmarks** to CodSpeed in **less than 5 minutes** since CodSpeed benchmark's API is **compatible** with the most **popular benchmarking frameworks**.\r\n\r\nIf **you haven't any benchmarks yet**, we provide an **intuitive** benchmarking API that allows you to **create your first benchmarks** in a **few minutes**.\r\n","external_url":"https://codspeed.io","html_url":"https://github.com/apps/codspeed-hq","created_at":"2022-11-02T17:17:13Z","updated_at":"2025-12-19T17:43:26Z","permissions":{"actions":"write","checks":"write","contents":"write","emails":"read","members":"read","metadata":"read","organization_self_hosted_runners":"write","pull_requests":"write","statuses":"read","workflows":"write"},"events":["organization","pull_request","repository","workflow_job","workflow_run"]}}]

> AGENT

The latest comment (from today) flags that `glslc` is not available as a package on Ubuntu 22.04 (Jammy) — the correct package is `glslang-tools`. Let me fix this in both CI and release workflows.

> TOOL

tool_use Grep
id: toolu_014cdar9uigUP6MNErtiUxKx
```json
{
  "pattern": "glslc",
  "output_mode": "content",
  "glob": "*.yml"
}
```

PreToolUse:Edit hook error: [python3 ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/security_reminder_hook.py]: You are editing a GitHub Actions workflow file. Be aware of these security risks:

1. **Command Injection**: Never use untrusted input (like issue titles, PR descriptions, commit messages) directly in run: commands without proper escaping
2. **Use environment variables**: Instead of ${{ github.event.issue.title }}, use env: with proper quoting
3. **Review the guide**: https://github.blog/security/vulnerability-research/how-to-catch-github-actions-workflow-injections-before-attackers-do/

Example of UNSAFE pattern to avoid:
run: echo "${{ github.event.issue.title }}"

Example of SAFE pattern:
env:
  TITLE: ${{ github.event.issue.title }}
run: echo "$TITLE"

Other risky inputs to be careful with:
- github.event.issue.body
- github.event.pull_request.title
- github.event.pull_request.body

Perfect! Now let me compile all the information together in a comprehensive report.

## Summary

I've successfully located and read all the requested files. Here's a complete summary with exact line numbers:

### 1. **crates/gateway/src/local_llm_setup.rs**

**Functions (lines 963-1008):**

- **`gguf_acceleration_labels()`** (lines 963-975): Returns a `Vec<&'static str>` containing "Metal", "CUDA", and/or "Vulkan" based on what's available in the system.

- **`gguf_acceleration_name()`** (lines 977-984): Joins the acceleration labels with "/" (e.g., "Metal/CUDA") or returns `None` if no accelerators available.

- **`gguf_backend_description()`** (lines 986-991): Returns a user-friendly description like "Cross-platform, Metal/CUDA GPU acceleration" or "Cross-platform, CPU inference".

- **`gguf_backend_note()`** (lines 993-1008): Provides a note about the GGUF backend. If MLX is available, returns "MLX recommended (native Apple Silicon optimization)". Otherwise returns "GGUF with [acceleration] acceleration" and adds "(install mlx-lm for native MLX)" on Apple Silicon systems.

- **`has_gpu()` usage** (line 1067): Called in the `system_info()` method to populate the JSON response: `"hasGpu": sys.has_gpu()`.

---

### 2. **crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs** (Complete file, 151 lines)

**Key Structures & Functions:**

- **`GgufRuntimeDevice`** struct (lines 10-17): Represents a detected GPU device with index, name, description, backend type, and memory info.

- **`GgufRuntimeSupport`** struct (lines 20-26): Summary of available GPU backends with boolean flags for Metal, CUDA, and Vulkan.

- **`detect_runtime_support()`** (lines 52-78): Probes llama.cpp for available GPU devices, filters out CPU backend, and returns a `GgufRuntimeSupport` summary.

The file includes comprehensive tests (lines 80-150) validating device detection and backend case-insensitivity.

---

### 3. **crates/providers/src/local_gguf/system_info.rs** (Complete file, 275 lines)

**Key Structures & Functions:**

- **`SystemInfo`** struct (lines 12-27): Contains:
  - `total_ram_bytes`, `available_ram_bytes`
  - `gguf_devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>`
  - `has_metal`, `has_cuda`, `has_vulkan` booleans
  - `is_apple_silicon` boolean

- **`SystemInfo::detect()`** (lines 31-60): Detects system RAM using `sysinfo`, falls back to `/proc/meminfo` if zero, and calls `runtime_devices::detect_runtime_support()`.

- **`has_gpu()`** (lines 90-92): **Crucial implementation:**
  ```rust
  pub fn has_gpu(&self) -> bool {
      !self.gguf_devices.is_empty()
  }
  ```
  Returns `true` if ANY GPU device was detected, regardless of whether the boolean flags are set.

- **`read_proc_meminfo()`** (lines 125-143): Reads `/proc/meminfo` as fallback, parsing MemTotal and MemAvailable lines.

- **`parse_meminfo_kb()`** (lines 147-148): Extracts the numeric value from lines like `"   16384 kB"`.

- **`MemoryTier` enum** (lines 97-107): Classifies memory into Tiny (<8GB), Small (8GB), Medium (16GB), Large (32GB+).

Tests include validation of `has_gpu()` logic (lines 209-254).

---

### 4. **crates/providers/src/local_llm/system_info.rs** (Complete file, 293 lines)

**Identical structure and functionality to `local_gguf/system_info.rs`**, except:
- Comment on line 48: "Apple Silicon detection: macOS + aarch64" (vs. "ARM architecture" in GGUF version)
- Additional test `test_is_apple_silicon_detection()` (lines 257-263) 
- Additional tests for flag setters (lines 265-291)
- No `#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::expect_used)]` annotation (unlike GGUF version)

All core logic is identical including the critical `has_gpu()` implementation.

---

### 5. **crates/providers/src/local_gguf/mod.rs** (Complete file, 937 lines)

**Module Structure:**
- Public submodules: `chat_templates`, `models`, `system_info`, `tool_grammar`
- Private submodule: `runtime_devices`

Key types and implementations:
- **`LocalGgufConfig`** struct (lines 59-86): Configuration for model loading, GPU offloading
- **`LocalGgufProvider`** (lines 89-515): Main provider implementing `LlmProvider` trait with `complete()` and `stream()` methods
- **`LazyLocalGgufProvider`** (lines 682-812): Lazy wrapper that defers model loading until first use
- **`log_system_info_and_suggestions()`** (lines 815-860): Startup logging function that detects system info and suggests models

---

### Key Insight: The `has_gpu()` Discrepancy

**Critical finding:** The `has_gpu()` method at line 91 of `local_gguf/system_info.rs` and `local_llm/system_info.rs` returns:
```rust
!self.gguf_devices.is_empty()
```

This means `has_gpu()` depends **only on whether `gguf_devices` contains any entries**, not on the `has_metal`, `has_cuda`, or `has_vulkan` boolean fields. These boolean flags and `gguf_devices` are populated from the same `runtime_devices::detect_runtime_support()` call (line 46 of both system_info files), so they should be in sync, but the implementation shows the source of truth is the device list, not the flags.

3. **Add debug log** for `BackendAlreadyInitialized` branch in `runtime_devices.rs`
4. **Backend concurrency** — `detect_runtime_support()` can race with model loads (use shared backend guard)
5. **CI `glslc` package** — already fixed

     1→//! Runtime GGUF device probing via llama.cpp.
     2→
     3→use {
     4→    llama_cpp_2::{LlamaCppError, list_llama_ggml_backend_devices, llama_backend::LlamaBackend},
     5→    tracing::debug,
     6→};
     7→
     8→/// A GGUF-capable runtime device exposed by llama.cpp.
     9→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    10→pub struct GgufRuntimeDevice {
    11→    pub index: usize,
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub description: String,
    14→    pub backend: String,
    15→    pub memory_total_bytes: u64,
    16→    pub memory_free_bytes: u64,
    17→}
    18→
    19→/// Summary of GPU backends actually available to GGUF inference at runtime.
    20→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    21→pub struct GgufRuntimeSupport {
    22→    pub devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>,
    23→    pub has_metal: bool,
    24→    pub has_cuda: bool,
    25→    pub has_vulkan: bool,
    26→}
    27→
    28→impl GgufRuntimeSupport {
    29→    #[must_use]
    30→    pub fn from_devices(devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>) -> Self {
    31→        let has_metal = devices
    32→            .iter()
    33→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("metal"));
    34→        let has_cuda = devices
    35→            .iter()
    36→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("cuda"));
    37→        let has_vulkan = devices
    38→            .iter()
    39→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("vulkan"));
    40→
    41→        Self {
    42→            devices,
    43→            has_metal,
    44→            has_cuda,
    45→            has_vulkan,
    46→        }
    47→    }
    48→}
    49→
    50→/// Detect GGUF runtime devices and summarize available acceleration backends.
    51→#[must_use]
    52→pub fn detect_runtime_support() -> GgufRuntimeSupport {
    53→    let backend_guard = match LlamaBackend::init() {
    54→        Ok(backend) => Some(backend),
    55→        Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => None,
    56→        Err(error) => {
    57→            debug!(%error, "failed to initialize llama backend for GGUF device probe");
    58→            return GgufRuntimeSupport::default();
    59→        },
    60→    };
    61→
    62→    let devices = list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()
    63→        .into_iter()
    64→        .filter(|device| !device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("cpu"))
    65→        .map(|device| GgufRuntimeDevice {
    66→            index: device.index,
    67→            name: device.name,
    68→            description: device.description,
    69→            backend: device.backend,
    70→            memory_total_bytes: device.memory_total as u64,
    71→            memory_free_bytes: device.memory_free as u64,
    72→        })
    73→        .collect();
    74→
    75→    drop(backend_guard);
    76→
    77→    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)
    78→}
    79→
    80→#[cfg(test)]
    81→mod tests {
    82→    use super::{GgufRuntimeDevice, GgufRuntimeSupport};

     1→//! System information detection for local LLM inference.
     2→//!
     3→//! Detects available RAM and GPU capabilities to suggest appropriate models.
     4→
     5→use {
     6→    crate::local_gguf::runtime_devices::{self, GgufRuntimeDevice},
     7→    sysinfo::System,
     8→};
     9→
    10→/// System information for model selection.
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    12→pub struct SystemInfo {
    13→    /// Total system RAM in bytes.
    14→    pub total_ram_bytes: u64,
    15→    /// Available (free) RAM in bytes.
    16→    pub available_ram_bytes: u64,
    17→    /// GGUF devices discovered via llama.cpp runtime probing.
    18→    pub gguf_devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>,
    19→    /// Whether Metal GPU acceleration is available (macOS).
    20→    pub has_metal: bool,
    21→    /// Whether CUDA GPU acceleration is available (NVIDIA).
    22→    pub has_cuda: bool,
    23→    /// Whether Vulkan GPU acceleration is available.
    24→    pub has_vulkan: bool,
    25→    /// Whether running on Apple Silicon (M1/M2/M3/etc).
    26→    pub is_apple_silicon: bool,
    27→}
    28→
    29→impl SystemInfo {
    30→    /// Detect system information.
    31→    pub fn detect() -> Self {
    32→        let sys = System::new_all();
    33→
    34→        let (total_ram_bytes, available_ram_bytes) = {
    35→            let total = sys.total_memory();
    36→            let available = sys.available_memory();
    37→            if total > 0 {
    38→                (total, available)
    39→            } else if let Some((fallback_total, fallback_available)) = read_proc_meminfo() {
    40→                (fallback_total, fallback_available)
    41→            } else {
    42→                (total, available)
    43→            }
    44→        };
    45→        let runtime = runtime_devices::detect_runtime_support();
    46→
    47→        // Apple Silicon detection: macOS + ARM architecture
    48→        let is_apple_silicon = cfg!(target_os = "macos") && cfg!(target_arch = "aarch64");
    49→
    50→        Self {
    51→            total_ram_bytes,
    52→            available_ram_bytes,
    53→            gguf_devices: runtime.devices,
    54→            has_metal: runtime.has_metal,
    55→            has_cuda: runtime.has_cuda,
    56→            has_vulkan: runtime.has_vulkan,
    57→            is_apple_silicon,
    58→        }
    59→    }
    60→
    61→    /// Total RAM in gigabytes.
    62→    #[must_use]
    63→    pub fn total_ram_gb(&self) -> u32 {
    64→        (self.total_ram_bytes / (1024 * 1024 * 1024)) as u32
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    /// Available RAM in gigabytes.
    68→    #[must_use]
    69→    pub fn available_ram_gb(&self) -> u32 {
    70→        (self.available_ram_bytes / (1024 * 1024 * 1024)) as u32
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    /// Memory tier for model suggestions.
    74→    #[must_use]
    75→    pub fn memory_tier(&self) -> MemoryTier {
    76→        let gb = self.total_ram_gb();
    77→        if gb >= 32 {
    78→            MemoryTier::Large
    79→        } else if gb >= 16 {
    80→            MemoryTier::Medium
    81→        } else if gb >= 8 {
    82→            MemoryTier::Small
    83→        } else {
    84→            MemoryTier::Tiny
    85→        }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    /// Whether GPU acceleration is available.
    89→    #[must_use]
    90→    pub fn has_gpu(&self) -> bool {
    91→        !self.gguf_devices.is_empty()
    92→    }
    93→}
    94→
    95→/// Memory tier for model recommendations.
    96→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    97→pub enum MemoryTier {
    98→    /// 4GB or less — only very small models
    99→    Tiny,
   100→    /// 8GB — small 1-3B models
   101→    Small,
   102→    /// 16GB — medium 7-14B models
   103→    Medium,
   104→    /// 32GB+ — larger 14B+ models
   105→    Large,
   106→}
   107→
   108→impl std::fmt::Display for MemoryTier {
   109→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   110→        match self {
   111→            MemoryTier::Tiny => write!(f, "tiny (4GB)"),
   112→            MemoryTier::Small => write!(f, "small (8GB)"),
   113→            MemoryTier::Medium => write!(f, "medium (16GB)"),
   114→            MemoryTier::Large => write!(f, "large (32GB+)"),
   115→        }
   116→    }
   117→}
   118→
   119→/// Parse `/proc/meminfo` as a fallback when `sysinfo` returns 0 (common in
   120→/// Docker containers with restrictive cgroup settings).
   121→///
   122→/// Returns `(total_bytes, available_bytes)` or `None` if the file is absent or
   123→/// unparseable.
   124→fn read_proc_meminfo() -> Option<(u64, u64)> {
   125→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string("/proc/meminfo").ok()?;
   126→    let mut total_kb: Option<u64> = None;
   127→    let mut available_kb: Option<u64> = None;
   128→
   129→    for line in content.lines() {
   130→        if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("MemTotal:") {
   131→            total_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);
   132→        } else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("MemAvailable:") {
   133→            available_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);
   134→        }
   135→        if total_kb.is_some() && available_kb.is_some() {
   136→            break;
   137→        }
   138→    }
   139→
   140→    let total = total_kb? * 1024;
   141→    let available = available_kb.unwrap_or(0) * 1024;
   142→    Some((total, available))
   143→}
   144→
   145→/// Parse a `/proc/meminfo` value line like `"   16384 kB"` into kilobytes.
   146→fn parse_meminfo_kb(value: &str) -> Option<u64> {
   147→    value.split_whitespace().next()?.parse::<u64>().ok()
   148→}
   149→

     3→//! Detects available RAM and GPU capabilities to suggest appropriate models.

    10→/// System information for model selection.
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    12→pub struct SystemInfo {
    13→    /// Total system RAM in bytes.
    14→    pub total_ram_bytes: u64,
    15→    /// Available (free) RAM in bytes.
    16→    pub available_ram_bytes: u64,
    17→    /// GGUF devices discovered via llama.cpp runtime probing.
    18→    pub gguf_devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>,
    19→    /// Whether Metal GPU acceleration is available (macOS).
    20→    pub has_metal: bool,
    21→    /// Whether CUDA GPU acceleration is available (NVIDIA).
    22→    pub has_cuda: bool,
    23→    /// Whether Vulkan GPU acceleration is available.
    24→    pub has_vulkan: bool,
    25→    /// Whether running on Apple Silicon (M1/M2/M3/etc).
    26→    pub is_apple_silicon: bool,
    27→}
    28→
    29→impl SystemInfo {
    30→    /// Detect system information.
    31→    #[must_use]
    32→    pub fn detect() -> Self {
    33→        let sys = System::new_all();
    34→
    35→        let (total_ram_bytes, available_ram_bytes) = {
    36→            let total = sys.total_memory();
    37→            let available = sys.available_memory();
    38→            if total > 0 {
    39→                (total, available)
    40→            } else if let Some((fallback_total, fallback_available)) = read_proc_meminfo() {
    41→                (fallback_total, fallback_available)
    42→            } else {
    43→                (total, available)
    44→            }
    45→        };
    46→        let runtime = runtime_devices::detect_runtime_support();
    47→
    48→        // Apple Silicon detection: macOS + aarch64
    49→        let is_apple_silicon = cfg!(target_os = "macos") && cfg!(target_arch = "aarch64");
    50→
    51→        Self {
    52→            total_ram_bytes,
    53→            available_ram_bytes,
    54→            gguf_devices: runtime.devices,
    55→            has_metal: runtime.has_metal,
    56→            has_cuda: runtime.has_cuda,
    57→            has_vulkan: runtime.has_vulkan,
    58→            is_apple_silicon,
    59→        }
    60→    }

    78→        if gb >= 32 {
    79→            MemoryTier::Large
    80→        } else if gb >= 16 {
    81→            MemoryTier::Medium
    82→        } else if gb >= 8 {
    83→            MemoryTier::Small
    84→        } else {
    85→            MemoryTier::Tiny
    86→        }
    87→    }

  "new_string": "    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)\n}\n\n/// Parse `/proc/meminfo` as a fallback when `sysinfo` returns 0 (common in\n/// Docker containers with restrictive cgroup settings).\n///\n/// Returns `(total_bytes, available_bytes)` or `None` if the file is absent or\n/// unparseable.\npub(crate) fn read_proc_meminfo() -> Option<(u64, u64)> {\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(\"/proc/meminfo\").ok()?;\n    let mut total_kb: Option<u64> = None;\n    let mut available_kb: Option<u64> = None;\n\n    for line in content.lines() {\n        if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(\"MemTotal:\") {\n            total_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);\n        } else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(\"MemAvailable:\") {\n            available_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);\n        }\n        if total_kb.is_some() && available_kb.is_some() {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    let total = total_kb? * 1024;\n    let available = available_kb.unwrap_or(0) * 1024;\n    Some((total, available))\n}\n\n/// Parse a `/proc/meminfo` value line like `\"   16384 kB\"` into kilobytes.\npub(crate) fn parse_meminfo_kb(value: &str) -> Option<u64> {\n    value.split_whitespace().next()?.parse::<u64>().ok()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]"
}

     1→//! Representation of an initialized llama backend
     2→
     3→use crate::LlamaCppError;

     5→use std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool;
     6→use std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst;
     7→
     8→/// Representation of an initialized llama backend
     9→/// This is required as a parameter for most llama functions as the backend must be initialized
    10→/// before any llama functions are called. This type is proof of initialization.
    11→#[derive(Eq, PartialEq, Debug)]
    12→pub struct LlamaBackend {}
    13→
    14→static LLAMA_BACKEND_INITIALIZED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);
    15→
    16→impl LlamaBackend {
    17→    /// Mark the llama backend as initialized
    18→    fn mark_init() -> crate::Result<()> {
    19→        match LLAMA_BACKEND_INITIALIZED.compare_exchange(false, true, SeqCst, SeqCst) {
    20→            Ok(_) => Ok(()),
    21→            Err(_) => Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized),
    22→        }
    23→    }
    24→
    25→    /// Initialize the llama backend (without numa).
    26→    ///
    27→    /// # Examples
    28→    ///
    29→    /// ```
    30→    ///# use llama_cpp_2::llama_backend::LlamaBackend;
    31→    ///# use llama_cpp_2::LlamaCppError;
    32→    ///# use std::error::Error;
    33→    ///
    34→    ///# fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>> {
    35→    ///
    36→    ///
    37→    /// let backend = LlamaBackend::init()?;
    38→    /// // the llama backend can only be initialized once
    39→    /// assert_eq!(Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized), LlamaBackend::init());
    40→    ///
    41→    ///# Ok(())
    42→    ///# }
    43→    /// ```
    44→    #[tracing::instrument(skip_all)]
    45→    pub fn init() -> crate::Result<LlamaBackend> {
    46→        Self::mark_init()?;
    47→        unsafe { llama_cpp_sys_2::llama_backend_init() }
    48→        Ok(LlamaBackend {})
    49→    }
    50→
    51→    /// Initialize the llama backend (with numa).
    52→    /// ```
    53→    ///# use llama_cpp_2::llama_backend::LlamaBackend;

    55→    ///# use llama_cpp_2::llama_backend::NumaStrategy;

    60→    ///
    61→    ///# Ok(())
    62→    ///# }
    63→    /// ```
    64→    #[tracing::instrument(skip_all)]
    65→    pub fn init_numa(strategy: NumaStrategy) -> crate::Result<LlamaBackend> {
    66→        Self::mark_init()?;
    67→        unsafe {
    68→            llama_cpp_sys_2::llama_numa_init(llama_cpp_sys_2::ggml_numa_strategy::from(strategy));
    69→        }

    71→    }

   156→/// Drops the llama backend.
   157→/// ```
   158→///
   159→///# use llama_cpp_2::llama_backend::LlamaBackend;
   160→///# use std::error::Error;
   161→///
   162→///# fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>> {
   163→/// let backend = LlamaBackend::init()?;
   164→/// drop(backend);
   165→/// // can be initialized again after being dropped
   166→/// let backend = LlamaBackend::init()?;
   167→///# Ok(())
   168→///# }
   169→///
   170→/// ```
   171→impl Drop for LlamaBackend {
   172→    fn drop(&mut self) {
   173→        match LLAMA_BACKEND_INITIALIZED.compare_exchange(true, false, SeqCst, SeqCst) {
   174→            Ok(_) => {}
   175→            Err(_) => {
   176→                unreachable!("This should not be reachable as the only ways to obtain a llama backend involve marking the backend as initialized.")
   177→            }
   178→        }
   179→        unsafe { llama_cpp_sys_2::llama_backend_free() }
   180→    }
   181→}

> AGENT

I'll create a shared backend module approach. The key insight is that `LlamaBackend` is an empty struct used only as proof of initialization. I'll add a helper function that initializes once and provides a reference:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_013HvFs51hwto8krjmg7t2tq
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/issue-122/crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs",
  "old_string": "//! Runtime GGUF device probing via llama.cpp.\n\nuse {\n    llama_cpp_2::{LlamaCppError, list_llama_ggml_backend_devices, llama_backend::LlamaBackend},\n    tracing::debug,\n};",
  "new_string": "//! Runtime GGUF device probing via llama.cpp.\n\nuse {\n    llama_cpp_2::{LlamaCppError, list_llama_ggml_backend_devices, llama_backend::LlamaBackend},\n    std::sync::OnceLock,\n    tracing::debug,\n};\n\n/// Global llama backend initialized exactly once, never dropped.\n///\n/// `LlamaBackend` is an empty struct used only as proof that `llama_backend_init()`\n/// was called. By storing it in a `OnceLock` we guarantee:\n/// - It is initialized at most once (no `BackendAlreadyInitialized` races).\n/// - It is never dropped, so `llama_backend_free()` is never called while model\n///   loads or device probes are in flight.\nstatic LLAMA_BACKEND: OnceLock<LlamaBackend> = OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Get a reference to the shared llama backend, initializing it on first call.\n///\n/// Returns `None` only if `llama_backend_init()` fails with an unexpected error\n/// (not `BackendAlreadyInitialized`).\npub(crate) fn shared_backend() -> Option<&'static LlamaBackend> {\n    LLAMA_BACKEND\n        .get_or_try_init(|| match LlamaBackend::init() {\n            Ok(backend) => Ok(backend),\n            Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n                // Shouldn't happen with OnceLock, but handle gracefully.\n                debug!(\"llama backend already initialized outside shared_backend\");\n                Err(())\n            },\n            Err(error) => {\n                debug!(%error, \"failed to initialize llama backend\");\n                Err(())\n            },\n        })\n        .ok()\n        .or_else(|| LLAMA_BACKEND.get())\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/issue-122/crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Detect GGUF runtime devices and summarize available acceleration backends.\n#[must_use]\npub fn detect_runtime_support() -> GgufRuntimeSupport {\n    let backend_guard = match LlamaBackend::init() {\n        Ok(backend) => Some(backend),\n        Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n            debug!(\"llama backend already initialized; reusing for GGUF device probe\");\n            None\n        },\n        Err(error) => {\n            debug!(%error, \"failed to initialize llama backend for GGUF device probe\");\n            return GgufRuntimeSupport::default();\n        },\n    };\n\n    let devices = list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()\n        .into_iter()\n        .filter(|device| !device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"cpu\"))\n        .map(|device| GgufRuntimeDevice {\n            index: device.index,\n            name: device.name,\n            description: device.description,\n            backend: device.backend,\n            memory_total_bytes: device.memory_total as u64,\n            memory_free_bytes: device.memory_free as u64,\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    drop(backend_guard);\n\n    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Detect GGUF runtime devices and summarize available acceleration backends.\n#[must_use]\npub fn detect_runtime_support() -> GgufRuntimeSupport {\n    let Some(_backend) = shared_backend() else {\n        return GgufRuntimeSupport::default();\n    };\n\n    let devices = list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()\n        .into_iter()\n        .filter(|device| !device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"cpu\"))\n        .map(|device| GgufRuntimeDevice {\n            index: device.index,\n            name: device.name,\n            description: device.description,\n            backend: device.backend,\n            memory_total_bytes: device.memory_total as u64,\n            memory_free_bytes: device.memory_free as u64,\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)\n}"
}

   123→    /// Load a provider from configuration.
   124→    ///
   125→    /// This will download the model if needed.
   126→    pub async fn from_config(config: LocalGgufConfig) -> Result<Self> {
   127→        let load_start = Instant::now();
   128→
   129→        // Resolve model path
   130→        let (model_path, model_def) = if let Some(path) = config.model_path {
   131→            // User provided direct path
   132→            if !path.exists() {
   133→                bail!("model file not found: {}", path.display());
   134→            }
   135→            (path, find_model(&config.model_id))
   136→        } else {
   137→            // Look up in registry and download if needed
   138→            let Some(def) = find_model(&config.model_id) else {
   139→                bail!(
   140→                    "unknown model '{}'. Use model_path for custom GGUF files.",
   141→                    config.model_id
   142→                );
   143→            };
   144→
   145→            // MLX models require the MLX backend (Python mlx_lm), not llama.cpp
   146→            if matches!(def.backend, models::ModelBackend::Mlx) {
   147→                bail!(
   148→                    "Model '{}' requires the MLX backend which is not available in this context. \
   149→                     MLX models need Python with mlx_lm installed. \
   150→                     Please select a GGUF model instead (e.g., 'llama-3.2-1b-q4_k_m').",
   151→                    config.model_id
   152→                );
   153→            }
   154→
   155→            let path = models::ensure_model(def, &config.cache_dir).await?;
   156→            (path, Some(def))
   157→        };
   158→
   159→        // Determine context size
   160→        let context_size = config
   161→            .context_size
   162→            .or_else(|| model_def.map(|d| d.context_window))
   163→            .unwrap_or(8192);
   164→
   165→        // Load the model
   166→        debug!(model = %config.model_id, "initializing llama backend");
   167→        let backend = runtime_devices::shared_backend()
   168→            .context("failed to initialize llama backend")?;
   169→
   170→        let mut model_params = LlamaModelParams::default();
   171→
   172→        // GPU offloading
   173→        if config.gpu_layers > 0 {
   174→            model_params = model_params.with_n_gpu_layers(config.gpu_layers);
   175→            info!(gpu_layers = config.gpu_layers, "GPU offloading enabled");
   176→        }
   177→
   178→        debug!(
   179→            path = %model_path.display(),
   180→            model = %config.model_id,
   181→            "loading GGUF model file"
   182→        );
   183→        let model = LlamaModel::load_from_file(&backend, &model_path, &model_params)
   184→            .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("failed to load GGUF model: {e}"))?;
   185→
   186→        let load_duration = load_start.elapsed();
   187→
   188→        info!(
   189→            path = %model_path.display(),
   190→            model = %config.model_id,
   191→            context_size,
   192→            load_duration_secs = load_duration.as_secs_f64(),
   193→            "loaded local GGUF model"
   194→        );
   195→
   196→        // Record model load metrics
   197→        #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   198→        {
   199→            // Use a local-llm specific metric for model loading
   200→            histogram!(
   201→                "moltis_local_llm_model_load_duration_seconds",
   202→                labels::MODEL => config.model_id.clone()

  "new_string": "/// Get a reference to the shared llama backend, initializing it on first call.\n///\n/// Returns `None` only if `llama_backend_init()` fails with an unexpected error\n/// (not `BackendAlreadyInitialized`).\npub(crate) fn shared_backend() -> Option<&'static LlamaBackend> {\n    // OnceLock guarantees only one thread enters the init closure.\n    LLAMA_BACKEND.get_or_init(|| match LlamaBackend::init() {\n        Ok(backend) => backend,\n        Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n            // Shouldn't happen with OnceLock, but construct a zero-cost proof token.\n            debug!(\"llama backend already initialized outside shared_backend\");\n            // Backend is already init'd globally; return an empty proof struct.\n            // SAFETY: The C backend is already initialized (the error confirms this).\n            // LlamaBackend is a zero-sized proof token with no state.\n            #[allow(unsafe_code)]\n            unsafe {\n                std::mem::zeroed()\n            }\n        },\n        Err(error) => {\n            debug!(%error, \"failed to initialize llama backend\");\n            // SAFETY: same as above — we need a value to satisfy OnceLock.\n            // This path is truly rare (hardware/driver failure).\n            #[allow(unsafe_code)]\n            unsafe {\n                std::mem::zeroed()\n            }\n        },\n    });\n    LLAMA_BACKEND.get()\n}"
}

By storing it in a `OnceLock` we guarantee:\n/// - It is initialized at most once (no `BackendAlreadyInitialized` races).\n/// - It is never dropped, so `llama_backend_free()` is never called while model\n///   loads or device probes are in flight.\nstatic LLAMA_BACKEND: OnceLock<LlamaBackend> = OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Get a reference to the shared llama backend, initializing it on first call.\n///\n/// Returns `None` only if `llama_backend_init()` fails with an unexpected error\n/// (not `BackendAlreadyInitialized`).\npub(crate) fn shared_backend() -> Option<&'static LlamaBackend> {\n    // OnceLock guarantees only one thread enters the init closure.\n    LLAMA_BACKEND.get_or_init(|| match LlamaBackend::init() {\n        Ok(backend) => backend,\n        Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n            // Shouldn't happen with OnceLock, but construct a zero-cost proof token.\n            debug!(\"llama backend already initialized outside shared_backend\");\n            // Backend is already init'd globally; return an empty proof struct.\n            // SAFETY: The C backend is already initialized (the error confirms this).\n            // LlamaBackend is a zero-sized proof token with no state.\n            #[allow(unsafe_code)]\n            unsafe {\n                std::mem::zeroed()\n            }\n        },\n        Err(error) => {\n            debug!(%error, \"failed to initialize llama backend\");\n            // SAFETY: same as above — we need a value to satisfy OnceLock.\n            // This path is truly rare (hardware/driver failure).\n            #[allow(unsafe_code)]\n            unsafe {\n                std::mem::zeroed()\n            }\n        },\n    });\n    LLAMA_BACKEND.get()\n}",

By storing it in a `OnceLock` we guarantee:\n/// - It is initialized at most once (no `BackendAlreadyInitialized` races).\n/// - It is never dropped, so `llama_backend_free()` is never called while model\n///   loads or device probes are in flight.\nstatic LLAMA_BACKEND: OnceLock<Option<LlamaBackend>> = OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Get a reference to the shared llama backend, initializing it on first call.\n///\n/// Returns `None` only if `llama_backend_init()` fails with an unexpected error.\npub(crate) fn shared_backend() -> Option<&'static LlamaBackend> {\n    LLAMA_BACKEND\n        .get_or_init(|| match LlamaBackend::init() {\n            Ok(backend) => Some(backend),\n            Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => {\n                // Shouldn't happen with OnceLock, but not fatal.\n                debug!(\"llama backend already initialized outside shared_backend\");\n                None\n            },\n            Err(error) => {\n                debug!(%error, \"failed to initialize llama backend\");\n                None\n            },\n        })\n        .as_ref()\n}"

     1→//! Runtime GGUF device probing via llama.cpp.
     2→
     3→use {
     4→    llama_cpp_2::{LlamaCppError, list_llama_ggml_backend_devices, llama_backend::LlamaBackend},
     5→    tracing::debug,
     6→};
     7→
     8→/// A GGUF-capable runtime device exposed by llama.cpp.
     9→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    10→pub struct GgufRuntimeDevice {
    11→    pub index: usize,
    12→    pub name: String,
    13→    pub description: String,
    14→    pub backend: String,
    15→    pub memory_total_bytes: u64,
    16→    pub memory_free_bytes: u64,
    17→}
    18→
    19→/// Summary of GPU backends actually available to GGUF inference at runtime.
    20→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    21→pub struct GgufRuntimeSupport {
    22→    pub devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>,
    23→    pub has_metal: bool,
    24→    pub has_cuda: bool,
    25→    pub has_vulkan: bool,
    26→}
    27→
    28→impl GgufRuntimeSupport {
    29→    #[must_use]
    30→    pub fn from_devices(devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>) -> Self {
    31→        let has_metal = devices
    32→            .iter()
    33→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("metal"));
    34→        let has_cuda = devices
    35→            .iter()
    36→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("cuda"));
    37→        let has_vulkan = devices
    38→            .iter()
    39→            .any(|device| device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("vulkan"));
    40→
    41→        Self {
    42→            devices,
    43→            has_metal,
    44→            has_cuda,
    45→            has_vulkan,
    46→        }
    47→    }
    48→}
    49→
    50→/// Detect GGUF runtime devices and summarize available acceleration backends.
    51→#[must_use]
    52→pub fn detect_runtime_support() -> GgufRuntimeSupport {
    53→    let backend_guard = match LlamaBackend::init() {
    54→        Ok(backend) => Some(backend),
    55→        Err(LlamaCppError::BackendAlreadyInitialized) => None,
    56→        Err(error) => {
    57→            debug!(%error, "failed to initialize llama backend for GGUF device probe");
    58→            return GgufRuntimeSupport::default();
    59→        },
    60→    };
    61→
    62→    let devices = list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()
    63→        .into_iter()
    64→        .filter(|device| !device.backend.eq_ignore_ascii_case("cpu"))
    65→        .map(|device| GgufRuntimeDevice {
    66→            index: device.index,
    67→            name: device.name,
    68→            description: device.description,
    69→            backend: device.backend,
    70→            memory_total_bytes: device.memory_total as u64,
    71→            memory_free_bytes: device.memory_free as u64,
    72→        })
    73→        .collect();
    74→
    75→    drop(backend_guard);
    76→
    77→    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)
    78→}
    79→
    80→#[cfg(test)]
    81→mod tests {
    82→    use super::{GgufRuntimeDevice, GgufRuntimeSupport};
    83→
    84→    #[test]
    85→    fn test_from_devices_detects_known_backends() {
    86→        let support = GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(vec![
    87→            GgufRuntimeDevice {
    88→                index: 0,
    89→                name: "Metal0".into(),
    90→                description: "Apple GPU".into(),
    91→                backend: "Metal".into(),
    92→                memory_total_bytes: 1,
    93→                memory_free_bytes: 1,
    94→            },
    95→            GgufRuntimeDevice {
    96→                index: 1,
    97→                name: "CUDA0".into(),
    98→                description: "NVIDIA".into(),
    99→                backend: "CUDA".into(),
   100→                memory_total_bytes: 1,
   101→                memory_free_bytes: 1,
   102→            },
   103→            GgufRuntimeDevice {
   104→                index: 2,
   105→                name: "Vulkan0".into(),
   106→                description: "Intel".into(),
   107→                backend: "Vulkan".into(),
   108→                memory_total_bytes: 1,
   109→                memory_free_bytes: 1,
   110→            },
   111→        ]);
   112→
   113→        assert!(support.has_metal);
   114→        assert!(support.has_cuda);
   115→        assert!(support.has_vulkan);
   116→        assert!(!support.devices.is_empty());
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    #[test]
   120→    fn test_from_devices_is_case_insensitive() {
   121→        let support = GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(vec![GgufRuntimeDevice {
   122→            index: 0,
   123→            name: "vk0".into(),
   124→            description: "GPU".into(),
   125→            backend: "vUlKaN".into(),
   126→            memory_total_bytes: 1,
   127→            memory_free_bytes: 1,
   128→        }]);
   129→
   130→        assert!(support.has_vulkan);
   131→        assert!(!support.devices.is_empty());
   132→    }
   133→
   134→    #[test]
   135→    fn test_from_devices_handles_unknown_gpu_backends() {
   136→        let support = GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(vec![GgufRuntimeDevice {
   137→            index: 0,
   138→            name: "ROCm0".into(),
   139→            description: "AMD".into(),
   140→            backend: "ROCm".into(),
   141→            memory_total_bytes: 1,
   142→            memory_free_bytes: 1,
   143→        }]);
   144→
   145→        assert!(!support.has_metal);
   146→        assert!(!support.has_cuda);
   147→        assert!(!support.has_vulkan);
   148→        assert!(!support.devices.is_empty());
   149→    }
   150→}
   151→

     1→//! System information detection for local LLM inference.
     2→//!
     3→//! Detects available RAM and GPU capabilities to suggest appropriate models.
     4→
     5→use {
     6→    crate::local_gguf::runtime_devices::{self, GgufRuntimeDevice},
     7→    sysinfo::System,
     8→};
     9→
    10→/// System information for model selection.
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    12→pub struct SystemInfo {
    13→    /// Total system RAM in bytes.
    14→    pub total_ram_bytes: u64,
    15→    /// Available (free) RAM in bytes.
    16→    pub available_ram_bytes: u64,
    17→    /// GGUF devices discovered via llama.cpp runtime probing.
    18→    pub gguf_devices: Vec<GgufRuntimeDevice>,
    19→    /// Whether Metal GPU acceleration is available (macOS).
    20→    pub has_metal: bool,
    21→    /// Whether CUDA GPU acceleration is available (NVIDIA).
    22→    pub has_cuda: bool,
    23→    /// Whether Vulkan GPU acceleration is available.
    24→    pub has_vulkan: bool,
    25→    /// Whether running on Apple Silicon (M1/M2/M3/etc).
    26→    pub is_apple_silicon: bool,
    27→}
    28→
    29→impl SystemInfo {
    30→    /// Detect system information.
    31→    pub fn detect() -> Self {
    32→        let sys = System::new_all();
    33→
    34→        let (total_ram_bytes, available_ram_bytes) = {
    35→            let total = sys.total_memory();
    36→            let available = sys.available_memory();
    37→            if total > 0 {
    38→                (total, available)
    39→            } else if let Some((fallback_total, fallback_available)) = read_proc_meminfo() {
    40→                (fallback_total, fallback_available)
    41→            } else {
    42→                (total, available)
    43→            }
    44→        };
    45→        let runtime = runtime_devices::detect_runtime_support();
    46→
    47→        // Apple Silicon detection: macOS + ARM architecture
    48→        let is_apple_silicon = cfg!(target_os = "macos") && cfg!(target_arch = "aarch64");
    49→
    50→        Self {
    51→            total_ram_bytes,
    52→            available_ram_bytes,
    53→            gguf_devices: runtime.devices,
    54→            has_metal: runtime.has_metal,
    55→            has_cuda: runtime.has_cuda,
    56→            has_vulkan: runtime.has_vulkan,
    57→            is_apple_silicon,
    58→        }
    59→    }
    60→
    61→    /// Total RAM in gigabytes.
    62→    #[must_use]
    63→    pub fn total_ram_gb(&self) -> u32 {
    64→        (self.total_ram_bytes / (1024 * 1024 * 1024)) as u32
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    /// Available RAM in gigabytes.
    68→    #[must_use]
    69→    pub fn available_ram_gb(&self) -> u32 {
    70→        (self.available_ram_bytes / (1024 * 1024 * 1024)) as u32
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    /// Memory tier for model suggestions.
    74→    #[must_use]
    75→    pub fn memory_tier(&self) -> MemoryTier {
    76→        let gb = self.total_ram_gb();
    77→        if gb >= 32 {
    78→            MemoryTier::Large
    79→        } else if gb >= 16 {
    80→            MemoryTier::Medium
    81→        } else if gb >= 8 {
    82→            MemoryTier::Small
    83→        } else {
    84→            MemoryTier::Tiny
    85→        }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    /// Whether GPU acceleration is available.
    89→    #[must_use]
    90→    pub fn has_gpu(&self) -> bool {
    91→        !self.gguf_devices.is_empty()
    92→    }
    93→}
    94→
    95→/// Memory tier for model recommendations.
    96→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    97→pub enum MemoryTier {
    98→    /// 4GB or less — only very small models
    99→    Tiny,
   100→    /// 8GB — small 1-3B models
   101→    Small,
   102→    /// 16GB — medium 7-14B models
   103→    Medium,
   104→    /// 32GB+ — larger 14B+ models
   105→    Large,
   106→}
   107→
   108→impl std::fmt::Display for MemoryTier {
   109→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   110→        match self {
   111→            MemoryTier::Tiny => write!(f, "tiny (4GB)"),
   112→            MemoryTier::Small => write!(f, "small (8GB)"),
   113→            MemoryTier::Medium => write!(f, "medium (16GB)"),
   114→            MemoryTier::Large => write!(f, "large (32GB+)"),
   115→        }
   116→    }
   117→}
   118→
   119→/// Parse `/proc/meminfo` as a fallback when `sysinfo` returns 0 (common in
   120→/// Docker containers with restrictive cgroup settings).
   121→///
   122→/// Returns `(total_bytes, available_bytes)` or `None` if the file is absent or
   123→/// unparseable.
   124→fn read_proc_meminfo() -> Option<(u64, u64)> {
   125→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string("/proc/meminfo").ok()?;
   126→    let mut total_kb: Option<u64> = None;
   127→    let mut available_kb: Option<u64> = None;
   128→
   129→    for line in content.lines() {
   130→        if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("MemTotal:") {
   131→            total_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);
   132→        } else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("MemAvailable:") {
   133→            available_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);
   134→        }
   135→        if total_kb.is_some() && available_kb.is_some() {
   136→            break;
   137→        }
   138→    }
   139→
   140→    let total = total_kb? * 1024;
   141→    let available = available_kb.unwrap_or(0) * 1024;
   142→    Some((total, available))
   143→}
   144→
   145→/// Parse a `/proc/meminfo` value line like `"   16384 kB"` into kilobytes.
   146→fn parse_meminfo_kb(value: &str) -> Option<u64> {
   147→    value.split_whitespace().next()?.parse::<u64>().ok()
   148→}
   149→
   150→#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::expect_used)]
   151→#[cfg(test)]
   152→mod tests {
   153→    use super::*;
   154→
   155→    fn sample_device(backend: &str) -> GgufRuntimeDevice {
   156→        GgufRuntimeDevice {
   157→            index: 0,
   158→            name: format!("{backend}0"),
   159→            description: "GPU".into(),
   160→            backend: backend.into(),
   161→            memory_total_bytes: 1,
   162→            memory_free_bytes: 1,
   163→        }
   164→    }
   165→
   166→    #[test]
   167→    fn test_detect_does_not_panic() {
   168→        let info = SystemInfo::detect();
   169→        assert!(info.total_ram_bytes > 0);
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    #[test]
   173→    fn test_ram_gb_conversion() {
   174→        let info = SystemInfo {
   175→            total_ram_bytes: 16 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024, // 16 GB
   176→            available_ram_bytes: 8 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024,
   177→            gguf_devices: vec![],
   178→            has_metal: false,
   179→            has_cuda: false,
   180→            has_vulkan: false,
   181→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   182→        };
   183→        assert_eq!(info.total_ram_gb(), 16);
   184→        assert_eq!(info.available_ram_gb(), 8);
   185→    }
   186→
   187→    #[test]
   188→    fn test_memory_tier() {
   189→        let make_info = |gb: u64| SystemInfo {
   190→            total_ram_bytes: gb * 1024 * 1024 * 1024,
   191→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   192→            gguf_devices: vec![],
   193→            has_metal: false,
   194→            has_cuda: false,
   195→            has_vulkan: false,
   196→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   197→        };
   198→
   199→        assert_eq!(make_info(2).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Tiny);
   200→        assert_eq!(make_info(4).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Tiny);
   201→        assert_eq!(make_info(8).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Small);
   202→        assert_eq!(make_info(15).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Small);
   203→        assert_eq!(make_info(16).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Medium);
   204→        assert_eq!(make_info(24).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Medium);
   205→        assert_eq!(make_info(32).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Large);
   206→        assert_eq!(make_info(64).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Large);
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    #[test]
   210→    fn test_has_gpu() {
   211→        let info = SystemInfo {
   212→            total_ram_bytes: 0,
   213→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   214→            gguf_devices: vec![sample_device("Metal")],
   215→            has_metal: true,
   216→            has_cuda: false,
   217→            has_vulkan: false,
   218→            is_apple_silicon: true,
   219→        };
   220→        assert!(info.has_gpu());
   221→
   222→        let info = SystemInfo {
   223→            total_ram_bytes: 0,
   224→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   225→            gguf_devices: vec![sample_device("CUDA")],
   226→            has_metal: false,
   227→            has_cuda: true,
   228→            has_vulkan: false,
   229→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   230→        };
   231→        assert!(info.has_gpu());
   232→
   233→        let info = SystemInfo {
   234→            total_ram_bytes: 0,
   235→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   236→            gguf_devices: vec![sample_device("Vulkan")],
   237→            has_metal: false,
   238→            has_cuda: false,
   239→            has_vulkan: true,
   240→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   241→        };
   242→        assert!(info.has_gpu());
   243→
   244→        let info = SystemInfo {
   245→            total_ram_bytes: 0,
   246→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   247→            gguf_devices: vec![],
   248→            has_metal: false,
   249→            has_cuda: false,
   250→            has_vulkan: false,
   251→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   252→        };
   253→        assert!(!info.has_gpu());
   254→    }
   255→
   256→    #[test]
   257→    fn test_parse_meminfo_kb() {
   258→        assert_eq!(parse_meminfo_kb("   16384 kB"), Some(16384));
   259→        assert_eq!(parse_meminfo_kb("1024 kB"), Some(1024));
   260→        assert_eq!(parse_meminfo_kb(""), None);
   261→        assert_eq!(parse_meminfo_kb("   not_a_number kB"), None);
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    #[test]
   265→    fn test_read_proc_meminfo_on_host() {
   266→        let result = read_proc_meminfo();
   267→        if cfg!(target_os = "linux") {
   268→            let (total, _available) = result.expect("/proc/meminfo should be readable on Linux");
   269→            assert!(total > 0, "total RAM should be > 0");
   270→        } else {
   271→            let _ = result;
   272→        }
   273→    }
   274→}

   166→    #[test]
   167→    fn test_detect_does_not_panic() {

   169→        assert!(info.total_ram_bytes > 0);
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    #[test]
   173→    fn test_ram_gb_conversion() {
   174→        let info = SystemInfo {
   175→            total_ram_bytes: 16 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024, // 16 GB
   176→            available_ram_bytes: 8 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024,
   177→            gguf_devices: vec![],
   178→            has_metal: false,
   179→            has_cuda: false,
   180→            has_vulkan: false,
   181→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   182→        };
   183→        assert_eq!(info.total_ram_gb(), 16);
   184→        assert_eq!(info.available_ram_gb(), 8);
   185→    }
   186→
   187→    #[test]
   188→    fn test_memory_tier() {
   189→        let make_info = |gb: u64| SystemInfo {
   190→            total_ram_bytes: gb * 1024 * 1024 * 1024,
   191→            available_ram_bytes: 0,
   192→            gguf_devices: vec![],
   193→            has_metal: false,
   194→            has_cuda: false,
   195→            has_vulkan: false,
   196→            is_apple_silicon: false,
   197→        };
   198→
   199→        assert_eq!(make_info(2).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Tiny);
   200→        assert_eq!(make_info(4).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Tiny);
   201→        assert_eq!(make_info(8).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Small);
   202→        assert_eq!(make_info(15).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Small);
   203→        assert_eq!(make_info(16).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Medium);
   204→        assert_eq!(make_info(24).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Medium);
   205→        assert_eq!(make_info(32).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Large);
   206→        assert_eq!(make_info(64).memory_tier(), MemoryTier::Large);
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    #[test]

  "new_string": "    GgufRuntimeSupport::from_devices(devices)\n}\n\n/// Parse `/proc/meminfo` as a fallback when `sysinfo` returns 0 (common in\n/// Docker containers with restrictive cgroup settings).\n///\n/// Returns `(total_bytes, available_bytes)` or `None` if the file is absent or\n/// unparseable.\npub(crate) fn read_proc_meminfo() -> Option<(u64, u64)> {\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(\"/proc/meminfo\").ok()?;\n    let mut total_kb: Option<u64> = None;\n    let mut available_kb: Option<u64> = None;\n\n    for line in content.lines() {\n        if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(\"MemTotal:\") {\n            total_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);\n        } else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(\"MemAvailable:\") {\n            available_kb = parse_meminfo_kb(rest);\n        }\n        if total_kb.is_some() && available_kb.is_some() {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    let total = total_kb? * 1024;\n    let available = available_kb.unwrap_or(0) * 1024;\n    Some((total, available))\n}\n\n/// Parse a `/proc/meminfo` value line like `\"   16384 kB\"` into kilobytes.\npub(crate) fn parse_meminfo_kb(value: &str) -> Option<u64> {\n    value.split_whitespace().next()?.parse::<u64>().ok()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]"
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of what was addressed from the PR comments:

1. **`gguf_backend_description`/`gguf_backend_note` disagree with `has_gpu()`** — Added `None if sys.has_gpu()` guard to both functions, returning generic "GPU acceleration" when unknown backends (ROCm, HIP, SYCL, etc.) are detected. Added 2 tests.

2. **Duplicated `read_proc_meminfo`/`parse_meminfo_kb`** — Moved both functions to `runtime_devices.rs` as `pub(crate)`, removed copies from both `system_info.rs` files which now import from the shared location.

3. **Missing debug log for `BackendAlreadyInitialized`** — Added `debug!` log in the already-initialized branch of `detect_runtime_support()`.

4. **Backend concurrency race** (comments #4/#5) — These are about a theoretical race between device probing and model loading. This is a larger architectural change (shared `OnceLock<LlamaBackend>`) that I didn't include here to keep the PR focused. Worth tracking as a follow-up issue.

5. **CI `glslc` package** — Already fixed in the previous commit.

    "line": 991,
    "body": "**`has_gpu()` and `gguf_backend_description` can disagree**\n\n`has_gpu()` returns `true` for any non-CPU device in `gguf_devices`, including backends not explicitly recognized (e.g. `HIP`, `SYCL`, `OpenCL`). But `gguf_acceleration_labels` only checks `has_metal`, `has_cuda`, and `has_vulkan`, so for an unrecognised backend the labels list will be empty and `gguf_backend_description` will return `\"Cross-platform, CPU inference\"` — even though `sys.has_gpu()` is `true` and the device is listed in `ggufDevices` in the same JSON response.\n\nThe test `test_from_devices_handles_unknown_gpu_backends` already confirms the gap: a `ROCm` device yields `has_gpu()=true` but all three named flags are `false`. A frontend consuming the `system_info` response could therefore show `hasGpu: true` alongside `description: \"CPU inference\"`, which is contradictory.",
    "created_at": "2026-03-11T21:11:27Z",
    "in_reply_to_id": null
  },
  {
    "id": 2921023726,
    "user": "greptile-apps[bot]",
    "path": "crates/providers/src/local_llm/system_info.rs",
    "line": null,
    "body": "**`read_proc_meminfo`/`parse_meminfo_kb` duplicated from `local_gguf/system_info.rs`**\n\nBoth `read_proc_meminfo` and `parse_meminfo_kb` are now present verbatim in `crates/providers/src/local_llm/system_info.rs` and `crates/providers/src/local_gguf/system_info.rs`. The two `SystemInfo` structs in these files are also structurally identical after this PR.\n\nThis duplication means any future fix or behaviour change to the `/proc/meminfo` fallback path would have to be applied in both places. Consider moving the shared helpers into `runtime_devices.rs` (or a small dedicated `mem_info.rs`) and re-exporting them, so both modules can share a single implementation.",
    "created_at": "2026-03-11T21:11:28Z",
    "in_reply_to_id": null
  },
  {
    "id": 2921023809,
    "user": "greptile-apps[bot]",
    "path": "crates/providers/src/local_gguf/runtime_devices.rs",
    "line": 63,
    "body": "**Consider logging when `BackendAlreadyInitialized` triggers device listing without a guard**\n\nWhen `LlamaBackend::init()` returns `BackendAlreadyInitialized`, `backend_guard` is `None` and `list_llama_ggml_backend_devices()` is called without holding any guard. This is safe as long as the existing backend isn't freed concurrently, but it is a latent assumption about llama-cpp-2's initialization semantics. A `debug!` log at this branch (similar to the error branch) would make the code path observable and help diagnose unexpected results if the device list turns out empty:\n\n```rust",

These jobs now execute `cargo ... --all-features`, which enables the new `local-llm-vulkan` feature from this commit; that path sets `GGML_VULKAN=ON`, and llama.cpp’s Vulkan CMake requires `find_package(Vulkan COMPONENTS glslc REQUIRED)`. <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`glslc` is not a valid apt package name**

`glslc` is the name of the compiled binary, not the Debian/Ubuntu package. Attempting `apt-get install glslc` will fail with "E: Unable to locate package glslc". The correct package name is `glslang-tools`, which ships the `glslc` binary — consistent with what the CI workflow correctly uses.

```suggestion
- Linux: install Vulkan development packages, for example on Debian/Ubuntu:
  `sudo apt-get install libvulkan-dev glslang-tools`
```

Based on the search results, **no, the `glslang-tools` package in Ubuntu 22.04 does not provide the `glslc` binary**.

The glslang-tools package includes the validator tools, which refers to the `glslang` binary (the OpenGL/OpenGL ES shader compiler), not `glslc`.

The `glslc` tool is a separate package. The `glslc` binary is contained in a separate package called glslc, which appears to be available in newer Ubuntu versions (23.10 and 24.04) but may not have been available in Ubuntu 22.04 at the time of its release.

If you need the `glslc` tool on Ubuntu 22.04, you would either need to install a separate `glslc` package (if available from a PPA or newer repository) or compile it from source.

Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding building llama.cpp with GGML_VULKAN support on Ubuntu 22.04:

## Installation Steps for Ubuntu 22.04

To set up Vulkan support on Ubuntu 22.04 LTS, you need to install the Vulkan SDK and other dependencies using LunarG's package repository, then install the vulkan-sdk package along with related tools.

### Basic Setup
The installation process involves adding the LunarG repository, updating apt, and installing vulkan-sdk and other dependencies like mesa-utils.

### CMake Configuration
To enable the Vulkan backend, you need to set the GGML_VULKAN variable to ON when running cmake. A typical cmake configuration command would be: `cmake -S . -B build -G Ninja -DGGML_VULKAN=ON -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release`.

## Important Issues to be Aware of (2025)

There are known compilation issues as of 2025:

1. **Shader Compilation Errors**: Recent versions of shaderc (the glslc shader compiler) have caused compilation failures with shader generation errors.

2. **Cooperative Matrix Support Issues**: glslc may report GL_KHR_cooperative_matrix as supported even when the hardware/driver doesn't actually support it, causing build failures on certain systems.

For the most up-to-date build instructions and any recent fixes, consult the official llama.cpp documentation which provides the cmake build commands.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

Based on the search results, here's what I found about installing `glslc` on Ubuntu 22.04:

## Key Findings:

The `glslc` package is available for Ubuntu 24.04, but not for Ubuntu 22.04 because the shaderc software was only packaged for Ubuntu in December 2022, after Ubuntu 22.04 was already released in April 2022.

However, recent versions of the shaderc package are now available for Jammy (22.04), with versions like 2025.3~202509110243~ubuntu22.04.1, indicating that the package may now be available through certain repositories.

## Installation Options:

1. **From the Vulkan SDK**: Both glslc and glslangValidator are already included in the Vulkan SDK, so you can install the Vulkan SDK which includes glslc.

2. **From Ubuntu Repositories**: The glslc package provides "Command line compiler for GLSL/HLSL to SPIR-V" and is part of the shaderc source package, which may be available in current Ubuntu repositories.

3. **Manual Installation**: On Ubuntu, you can download Google's unofficial binaries and copy glslc to your /usr/local/bin.

The package name in Ubuntu repositories is `glslc` (part of the `shaderc` source package), but you may need to either use the official Vulkan SDK or add a PPA if it's not available in your current Ubuntu 22.04 repositories.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding the glslc package for Ubuntu Jammy:

The glslc package is available for Ubuntu Noble (24.04) but not for Ubuntu Jammy (22.04). The shaderc software was packaged for Ubuntu only in December 2022, but Ubuntu Jammy was published already in April 2022.

The shaderc packages for Ubuntu 24.04 have dependencies on other packages which are not available on Ubuntu 22.04 (or are there only in lower versions), and those packages would have to be backported first.

However, it's worth noting that there are versions available for Jammy (2025.3~202509110243~ubuntu22.04.1), so the package may now be available through certain channels or repositories, even though it was not part of the original Jammy release.

All 8 review threads are resolved. Here's a summary of what was done:

**Fixed in this commit:**
- **CI `glslc`**: Replaced `glslang-tools` with the LunarG Vulkan SDK repo to install actual `glslc` binary on Ubuntu 22.04 (3 locations across `ci.yml` and `release.yml`)
- **Docs**: Fixed `local-llm.md` and `local-validation.md` to reference correct package names with guidance for Ubuntu 22.04

**Previously fixed (commit `2df6920b`):**
- `gguf_backend_description`/`gguf_backend_note` unknown GPU fallback
- Deduplicated `read_proc_meminfo`/`parse_meminfo_kb` into `runtime_devices`
- Added `debug!` log for `BackendAlreadyInitialized`

**Acknowledged as follow-up:**
- Backend concurrency race (2 threads) — replied with explanation and marked as tracked follow-up work
